sys.path.insert(0,\"/app/analysis\")\nfrom load import meta, series\nimport datetime as dt\n\n# unrelated transient windows identified: isolated short excursions w/ rapid return\nMASK={\n 'STEAM_PRES_01':[(dt.datetime(2024,1,15,19,55),dt.datetime(2024,1,15,20,30))],\n 'TURB_OUTPUT_01':[(dt.datetime(2024,1,15,17,55),dt.datetime(2024,1,15,18,30))],\n 'COOLING_PRES_01':[(dt.datetime(2024,1,16,0,5),dt.datetime(2024,1,16,0,40))],\n}\n\ndef fit(xs,ys,tc):\n    n=len(xs); cols=[[1.0]*n,xs,[max(0.0,x-tc) for x in xs]]\n    A=[[sum(cols[i][k]*cols[j][k] for k in range(n)) for j in range(3)] for i in range(3)]\n    b=[sum(cols[i][k]*ys[k] for k in range(n)) for i in range(3)]\n    M=[A[i][:]+[b[i]] for i in range(3)]\n    for i in range(3):\n        p=max(range(i,3),key=lambda r:abs(M[r][i]))\n        if abs(M[p][i])<1e-12: return None,1e30\n        M[i],M[p]=M[p],M[i]\n        for r in range(3):\n            if r!=i:\n                f=M[r][i]/M[i][i]\n                for c in range(i,4): M[r][c]-=f*M[i][c]\n    sol=[M[i][3]/M[i][i] for i in range(3)]\n    sse=sum((ys[k]-(sol[0]+sol[1]*xs[k]+sol[2]*max(0.0,xs[k]-tc)))**2 for k in range(n))\n    return sol,sse\n\ndef run(W0,W1,label):\n    print(f\"\\n===== window {W0.strftime('%m-%d %H:%M')} .. {W1.strftime('%m-%d %H:%M')} ({label}) =====\")\n    res=[]\n    for sid,m in meta.items():\n        pts=[(t,v) for t,v in series[sid] if W0<=t<=W1\n             and not any(a<=t<=b for a,b in MASK.get(sid,[]))]\n        xs=[(t-W0).total_seconds()/60 for t,v in pts]; Alarms fired TURBINE (02:10Z) → COOLING (02:38Z) → STEAM_GEN (03:42Z) → PRIMARY_LOOP (05:56Z) → CORE (11:26Z). The CORE sensors have the longest response delays (up to 8 min) and the widest normal bands, so the subsystem that failed *first* tripped its alarms *last*. Operator actions follow the alarms, so they mislead the same way.\n\n**Three corrections applied:**\n\n1. **Timebase.** The loggers record local time with different UTC offsets (`sensors_01`=0, `sensors_02`=+2, `sensors_03`=−1) — a ±3 h skew, far larger than the ~80 min cascade. I verified the sign against `alarms.csv` (already in Z): with it, every sensor leaves its normal band before its alarm fires; with the opposite sign CORE and TURBINE alarms would precede their band crossings by hours.\n\n2. **Sensor lag.** Subtracted each sensor's `response_delay_minutes` from the fitted observation breakpoint.\n\n3. **Operator contamination.** Fit window ends 02:45Z, before the first action (EMERGENCY_SHUTDOWN, 02:52Z).\n\nI also masked three isolated transients unrelated to the cascade — the TURB_OUTPUT_01 dip at 15 18:00Z, the STEAM_PRES_01 step at 15 20:00Z, and the COOLING_PRES_01 spike at 16 00:10Z (which alarmed 2 h before the real cooling onset and is the most tempting decoy).\n\n**Physical onsets (UTC):** CORE 23:59 → PRIMARY_LOOP 00:17 → STEAM_GEN 00:45 → COOLING 01:04 → TURBINE 01:17.\n\nWithin each subsystem all four sensors land within a few minutes of each other, while the gaps *between* subsystems are 13–28 min — clean separation with no overlap. The order held across three fit windows, two independent estimators (rolling-mean departure and ramp back-extrapolation), and bootstrap resampling.
